L^{\text{drift}}(N)=\sum_{i=1}^{M}r_{i}(0)\,e^{-\tfrac{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{N}\tfrac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du},\quad L^{\text{noise}}(N)=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\int_{0}^{N}e^{-\tfrac{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{z}^{N}\tfrac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du}\,f(z)^{2}\,dz.

(27)

Then

L(N)=\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}+L^{\text{drift}}(N)+L^{\text{noise}}(N),

(28)

and we will analyze
\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}+L^{\text{drift}}(N)
and
L^{\text{noise}}(N)
separately.

Figure
19
shows dynamics of three terms
\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}
,
L^{\text{drift}}(N)
,
L^{\text{noise}}(N)
referring each as Approx, Drift, Noise.
The right plot in Figure
19
validates the equality in (
28
).

Figure 19:
Dynamics of Drift and Noise.

Left: the purple curve is the 80
\%
confidence interval of the true signSGD trajectory, while the blue curve is the numerical ODE solution. The yellow, orange, and green curves correspond to the approximation, drift, and noise terms in (
28
).
Right: the red curve shows the sum of these three terms, matching both the true trajectory and the ODE solution. Parameters:
\alpha=1.0
,
\beta=0
,
\gamma_{0}=0.003
,
f(z)=1
,
M=200
,
d=800
.

E.2.1
Transformation of the Drift Term and Approximation Error

Let

Q(z)\;=\;\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{z}\frac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du,\qquad\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}\;=\;{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}}\operatorname{diag}({\bm{S}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}})^{-1/2}{\bm{S}}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}.

Then

{\bm{K}}\,\overline{{\bm{K}}}^{p}\;=\;{\bm{S}}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}^{p}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}}

holds.

Define

{\bm{A}}\;=\;{\bm{H}}^{1/2}e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)}{\bm{H}}^{1/2},\qquad{\bm{u}}\;=\;{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{\theta}}^{*}-{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\;=\;{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{w}}^{*}.

From
{\bm{S}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}=0
we get

\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}({\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp})=0,

and this implies

e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)}({\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp})=e^{0}({\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp})={\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}.

Thus,

{\bm{A}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}={\bm{H}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp},\qquad{\bm{w}}_{\perp}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{A}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}={\bm{w}}_{\perp}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp},

and

{\bm{u}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{A}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}={\bm{u}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}=({\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{S}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}-{\bm{w}}_{\perp}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}=-\,{\bm{w}}_{\perp}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}.

Using these identities, we can convert the drift term as follows:

\displaystyle L^{\text{drift}}(N)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{M}r_{i}(0)\cdot e^{-\lambda_{i}(\overline{{\bm{K}}})Q(N)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}({\bm{K}}{\bm{u}}_{i}\otimes{\bm{w}}_{i})({\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*})\cdot e^{-\lambda_{i}(\overline{{\bm{K}}})Q(N)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{M}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}\Bigl(({\bm{K}}{\bm{u}}_{i}\otimes{\bm{w}}_{i})\cdot e^{-\lambda_{i}(\overline{{\bm{K}}})Q(N)}\Bigr)({\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*})

\displaystyle=({\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{K}}e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}Q(N)}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*})

\displaystyle=({\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{S}}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}\,\bigl({\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{S}}e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}Q(N)}\bigr)({\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*})

\displaystyle=({\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{S}}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}\,\bigl(e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{S}}\bigr)({\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*})

\displaystyle=({\bm{u}}+{\bm{w}}_{\perp})^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{A}}({\bm{u}}+{\bm{w}}_{\perp})

\displaystyle={\bm{u}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{A}}{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{A}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}+{\bm{w}}_{\perp}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{A}}{\bm{u}}+{\bm{w}}_{\perp}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{A}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}

\displaystyle={\bm{u}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{u}}-{\bm{w}}_{\perp}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}-{\bm{w}}_{\perp}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}+{\bm{w}}_{\perp}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}

\displaystyle={\bm{u}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{u}}-\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}.

Drift term plus approximation error.

Adding the approximation error gives

\displaystyle L^{\text{drift}}(N)+\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle={\bm{u}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{u}}

\displaystyle=\Big\langle e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)},\;\bigl({\bm{H}}^{1/2}({\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{w}}^{*})\bigr)^{\otimes 2}\Big\rangle.

Also we assume
{\bm{\theta}}_{0}=0
, then

\big\langle e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)},\;({\bm{H}}^{1/2}({\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{\theta}}_{0}-{\bm{w}}^{*}))^{\otimes 2}\big\rangle=\big\langle e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)},\;({\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}^{*})^{\otimes 2}\big\rangle.

In the next subsection, we will describe how to apply a deterministic approximation,
similar to
32
, to the following term:

\mathcal{H}\;:=\;\big\langle e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)},\,{\bm{v}}^{\otimes 2}\big\rangle,

where
{\bm{v}}:={\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

E.2.2
Deterministic Approximation

Note that we assume
d\geq rM
for some
r>1
, and let
d/M\to(1,\infty]
as
d,M\to\infty
when
2\alpha>1
, and
d/M\to(1,\infty)
when
2\alpha<1
.
In our setup,
S\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times d}
have i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1/M)
entries, and we will write the
k
th column of
S^{\mathsf{T}}
as
\frac{1}{\sqrt{M}}{\bm{s}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
; columns are independent.

Define

{\bm{y}}_{k}:={\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{s}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\qquad a_{k}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tfrac{1}{M}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{y}}_{k}}}=\frac{\sqrt{M}}{\sqrt{{\bm{s}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{s}}_{k}}}>0.

The unnormalized baseline and the column–normalized matrices are

\widehat{\bm{K}}:={\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{S}}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{k=1}^{M}{\bm{y}}_{k}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}},\qquad\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}:={\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}}\,\operatorname{diag}({\bm{S}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}})^{-1/2}{\bm{S}}{\bm{H}}^{1/2}=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{k=1}^{M}a_{k}\,{\bm{y}}_{k}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}.

For
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}:=\{z:\Im z>0\}
, define the resolvents

{\bm{L}}(z):=(\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}-z{\bm{I}})^{-1},\qquad{\bm{R}}^{(k)}(z):=\left(\frac{1}{M}\sum_{\ell\neq k}a_{\ell}{\bm{y}}_{\ell}{\bm{y}}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{T}}-z{\bm{I}}\right)^{-1}.

Note that

{\bm{y}}_{k}{\bm{B}}\,{\bm{y}}_{k}\approx\Tr({\bm{H}}{\bm{B}})

for matrix
{\bm{B}}
.
In particular,

{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{y}}_{k}\approx\Tr{\bm{H}},\qquad a_{k}\approx\frac{\sqrt{M}}{\sqrt{\Tr{\bm{H}}}}.

Also note that

a_{k}\,{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{R}}^{(k)}\,{\bm{y}}_{k}\approx\frac{\sqrt{M}}{\sqrt{\Tr{\bm{H}}}}\cdot\Tr({\bm{H}}\,{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{R}}^{(k)}),

and

a_{k}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}^{(k)}{\bm{y}}_{k}\approx\frac{\sqrt{M}}{\sqrt{\Tr{\bm{H}}}}\Tr({\bm{H}}\,{\bm{R}}^{(k)}).

By the Sherman–Morrison expansion,

{\bm{R}}={\bm{R}}^{(k)}-\frac{M^{-1}a_{k}\,{\bm{R}}^{(k)}{\bm{y}}_{k}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}^{(k)}}{1+M^{-1}a_{k}\,{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}^{(k)}{\bm{y}}_{k}}.

Multiplying on the left by
{\bm{R}}
and sandwiching with
{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}(\cdot){\bm{y}}_{k}
, we get

\displaystyle a_{k}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{y}}_{k}

\displaystyle=a_{k}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{R}}^{(k)}{\bm{y}}_{k}-\frac{M^{-1}a_{k}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{R}}^{(k)}{\bm{y}}_{k}\cdot a_{k}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}^{(k)}{\bm{y}}_{k}}{1+M^{-1}a_{k}\,{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}^{(k)}{\bm{y}}_{k}}.

Now we will replace terms on the right side by

a_{k}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{R}}^{(k)}{\bm{y}}_{k}\approx\frac{\sqrt{M}}{\sqrt{\Tr{\bm{H}}}}\Tr({\bm{H}}\,{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{R}}^{(k)}),

and

a_{k}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}^{(k)}{\bm{y}}_{k}\approx\frac{\sqrt{M}}{\sqrt{\Tr{\bm{H}}}}\Tr({\bm{H}}\,{\bm{R}}^{(k)}).

Thus

a_{k}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{y}}_{k}\approx\frac{\tfrac{\sqrt{M}}{\sqrt{\Tr{\bm{H}}}}\Tr({\bm{H}}\,{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{R}}^{(k)})}{1+M^{-1}\tfrac{\sqrt{M}}{\sqrt{\Tr{\bm{H}}}}\Tr({\bm{H}}\,{\bm{R}}^{(k)})}.

Replacing
{\bm{R}}^{(k)}
by
{\bm{R}}
and averaging over
k
, we obtain

\frac{1}{M}\sum_{k=1}^{M}a_{k}{\bm{y}}_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{y}}_{k}\approx\frac{p_{d}\Tr({\bm{H}}\,{\bm{R}}\,{\bm{R}})}{1+M^{-1}p_{d}\Tr({\bm{H}}\,{\bm{R}})},\qquad p_{d}:=\frac{\sqrt{M}}{\sqrt{\Tr{\bm{H}}}}.

It implies

\operatorname{Tr}({\bm{R}}({\bm{R}}^{-1}+z{\bm{I}}){\bm{R}})\approx\frac{p_{d}\Tr({\bm{R}}\,{\bm{H}}\,{\bm{R}})}{1+M^{-1}p_{d}\Tr({\bm{H}}\,{\bm{R}})}.

This implies

{\bm{L}}(z)^{-1}+z{\bm{I}}\approx\frac{p_{d}}{1+M^{-1}p_{d}\Tr({\bm{H}}\,{\bm{L}}(z))}{\bm{H}}.

Let

m(z/p_{d})=\frac{1}{1+M^{-1}p_{d}\Tr({\bm{H}}\,{\bm{L}}(z))}.

Then

{\bm{L}}(z)\approx(-z{\bm{I}}+p_{d}m(z/p_{d}){\bm{H}})^{-1}.

Thus

(\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}-z{\bm{I}})^{-1}\approx(-z{\bm{I}}+p_{d}m(z/p_{d}){\bm{H}})^{-1}.

Therefore,

m(z)=\frac{1}{1+M^{-1}p_{d}\Tr({\bm{H}}\,{\bm{R}}(p_{d}z))}\approx\frac{1}{1+M^{-1}\Tr({\bm{H}}(-z{\bm{I}}+m(z){\bm{H}})^{-1})}

holds. This fixed–point equation is identical to the one in
32
.

Contour representation.

Let
{\bm{v}}:={\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and consider

\mathcal{H}:=\left\langle e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)},\,{\bm{v}}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle.

For any analytic
g
on a contour
\Gamma_{2}
enclosing
\mathrm{Spec}(\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1})
,

g(\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1})=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{2}}g(z)(\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}-z{\bm{I}})^{-1}\,dz.

We prove

c_{1}\,M^{\min(0.5,\alpha)}\,I\;\preceq\;\operatorname{diag}({\bm{S}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}})^{-1/2}\;\preceq\;c_{2}\,M^{\min(0.5,\alpha)}\,I

in Section
K.4
.
It leads to

c_{1}\,M^{\min(0.5,\alpha)}\widehat{\bm{K}}\preceq\overline{{\bm{K}}}\preceq c_{2}\,M^{\min(0.5,\alpha)}\widehat{\bm{K}}.

\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}
has eigenvalues scaled by
M^{\min(0.5,\alpha)}
compared to
\widehat{\bm{K}}
excluding constant.
Note that
p_{d}\eqsim M^{\min(0.5,\alpha)}
.
So, there exists a contour
\Gamma_{2}
enclosing the spectrum of
\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}
, and its
1/p_{d}
–scaled version
\Gamma
encloses the spectrum of
\widehat{{\bm{K}}}
.

Taking
g(z)=e^{-Q(N)z}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{2}}e^{-Q(N)z}\left\langle(\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}-z{\bm{I}})^{-1},\,{\bm{v}}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle\,dz

\displaystyle\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{2}}e^{-Q(N)z}\left\langle(-z{\bm{I}}+p_{d}m(z/p_{d}){\bm{H}})^{-1},\,{\bm{v}}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle\,dz

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma}e^{-p_{d}Q(N)z}\left\langle(-z{\bm{I}}+m(z){\bm{H}})^{-1},\,{\bm{v}}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle\,dz.

Let
\mathcal{R}(z)=(-z{\bm{I}}+m(z){\bm{H}})^{-1}
, then our objective converts to

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma}e^{-p_{d}Q(N)z}\left\langle\mathcal{R}(z),\,{\bm{v}}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle\,dz.

E.2.3
Final Transformation Result

32
evaluate the contour integrals with
\mathcal{R}(z)
.
When
\alpha<0.5
or
\beta<0.5
, they show

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\Gamma}\bigl(1-2\gamma Bz+\gamma^{2}B(B+1)z^{2}\bigr)^{r}\,\left\langle\mathcal{L}(z),\,v^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle\,dz

\displaystyle\;\eqsim\;M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}

\displaystyle\quad+\;(2\gamma Br)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}.

(29)

When
\alpha>0.5
and
\beta>0.5
, they obtained

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\Gamma}\bigl(1-2\gamma Bz+\gamma^{2}B(B+1)z^{2}\bigr)^{r}\,\left\langle\mathcal{L}(z),\,v^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle\,dz

\displaystyle\;\eqsim\;M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}

\displaystyle\quad+\;(2\gamma Br)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}

\displaystyle\quad+\;M^{-1}\,(2\gamma Br)^{-2+\tfrac{1}{2\alpha}}.

(30)

For the case
\alpha<0.5
or
\beta<0.5
, applying a similar method to our objective yields

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\Gamma}e^{-p_{d}\,Q(N)z}\,\left\langle\mathcal{L}(z),\,v^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle\,dz

\displaystyle\;\eqsim\;M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}

\displaystyle\quad+\;\Bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q(N)\Bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}},

(31)

with details provided in Appendix
K.1
. Hence,

\displaystyle\left\langle e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)},\,({\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}^{*})^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\;\eqsim\;M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}

\displaystyle\quad+\;\Bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q(N)\Bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}.

(32)

For the case
\alpha>0.5
and
\beta>0.5
, a similar argument gives

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\Gamma}e^{-p_{d}\,Q(N)z}\,\left\langle\mathcal{L}(z),\,v^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle\,dz

\displaystyle\;\eqsim\;M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}

\displaystyle\quad+\;\Bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q(N)\Bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}

\displaystyle\quad+\;M^{-1}\,\Bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q(N)\Bigr)^{-1+\tfrac{1}{2\alpha}},

(33)

with details in Appendix
K.1
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\left\langle e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(N)},\,({\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}^{*})^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\;\eqsim\;M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}

\displaystyle\quad+\;\Bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q(N)\Bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}

\displaystyle\quad+\;M^{-1}\,\Bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q(N)\Bigr)^{-1+\tfrac{1}{2\alpha}}.

(34)

In summary, we obtain

L^{\text{drift}}(N)+\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}\;\eqsim\;M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}\;+\;\Bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q(N)\Bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}},

(35)

for
\alpha<0.5
or
\beta<0.5
, and

\displaystyle L^{\text{drift}}(N)+\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\;\eqsim\;M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}

\displaystyle\quad+\;\Bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q(N)\Bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}

\displaystyle\quad+\;M^{-1}\,\Bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q(N)\Bigr)^{-1+\tfrac{1}{2\alpha}},

(36)

for
\alpha>0.5
and
\beta>0.5
.

Figure
20
shows that our transformed result in (
35
) and (
36
) based on deterministic approximation match the true signSGD trajectory up to a constant factor.
When interpreting the figure, note that our analysis is asymptotic; hence, discrepancies may appear in the very early iterations.

Figure 20:
Verification of the deterministic approximation and drift-term transformation.

Left: the purple curve denotes the 80% confidence interval of the true signSGD trajectory, the blue curve represents the numerical ODE solution, and the yellow curve corresponds to the deterministic approximation after drift-term transformation in (
35
) and (
36
). Deterministic approximation matches the true trajectory up to a constant factor. It should be noted that our analysis is asymptotic, and thus, discrepancies may occur in the very early iterations.
Right: the ratio between the approximation and the true trajectory remains bounded by a constant factor, confirming the validity of our approach.
Parameters:
(\alpha,\beta)=(0.7,0.3),(1.0,0),(0.4,0.4),(0.7,1.1)
from top to bottom,
\gamma_{0}=0.003
,
f(z)=1
,
M=200
,
d=800
, 100 runs.

E.3
Constant Learning Rate: Proxy and Verification for the Case
\alpha<0.5
or
\beta<0.5
(Phase A)

Throughout this section, we set
f(z)\equiv 1
; hence

Q(N)=\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{L(u)}}.

Applying the drift/approximation-term transformation to the ODE solution yields the implicit relation

\displaystyle L(N)\;\eqsim\;\underbrace{M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,1-2\beta)}}_{\textbf{approx}}\;+\;\underbrace{\bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}}_{\textbf{drift}}\;

(37)

\displaystyle+\;\underbrace{\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\frac{du}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz}_{\textbf{noise}}.

(38)

E.3.1
Early Stage (Dominance of the Drift Term)

At
N=0
, the noise integral is
0
, the approximation term is independent of
N
, and the drift term is large and decreases with
N
. Thus, in the early phase,

L(N)\ \eqsim\ \bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}.

(39)

Since
Q(N)=\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{N}L(u)^{-1/2}\,du
, (
39
) is equivalent (up to absolute constants) to

L(N)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha}{\,2\alpha+2\beta-1\,}}\ \eqsim\ M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,\gamma_{0}\!\int_{0}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{L(u)}}.

(40)

To obtain a proxy profile, we replace
\eqsim
by equality in (
40
) and differentiate both sides:

-\frac{2\alpha}{\,2\alpha+2\beta-1\,}\,L(t)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha}{\,2\alpha+2\beta-1\,}-1}\,L^{\prime}(t)\;=\;M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,\gamma_{0}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{L(t)}}.

(41)

Solving (
41
) for
L^{\prime}(t)
and separating variables gives the separable ODE

\frac{dL}{dt}=-\kappa\,L^{\zeta},\qquad\zeta=\frac{2\alpha}{\,2\alpha+2\beta-1\,}+\frac{1}{2},\qquad\kappa=\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,\gamma_{0}.

Assuming
\zeta>1
(i.e.
2\alpha+2\beta<4\alpha+1
), we integrate to obtain

-\frac{L(t)^{-(\zeta-1)}}{\zeta-1}=-\kappa t+\text{constant}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad L(t)=\Bigl[(\zeta-1)\,\kappa\,t\Bigr]^{-1/(\zeta-1)}.

(42)

Substituting
\zeta=\tfrac{2\alpha}{\,2\alpha+2\beta-1\,}+\tfrac{1}{2}
and
\kappa=\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,\gamma_{0}
yields the early-phase proxy

L(N)\ \eqsim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N\bigr)^{-p},\qquad p:=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}.

(43)

By construction, (
43
) satisfies (
40
) (hence (
39
)) up to absolute constants.

E.3.2
Limit Stage (Stationary Analysis and Floor)

With
f\equiv 1
, the mode-wise ODE is

\frac{dp_{i}}{dt}=-\frac{4}{\pi\sqrt{P(t)}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\,p_{i}(t)+\frac{2\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\,V_{i}.

At stationarity,
p_{i}(t)\to s_{i}
and
P(t)\to L_{\infty}
, we must have

-\frac{4}{\pi\sqrt{L_{\infty}}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\,s_{i}+\frac{2\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\,V_{i}=0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad s_{i}=\frac{\gamma_{0}\sqrt{L_{\infty}}}{2\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}\,V_{i}=\frac{\gamma_{0}\sqrt{L_{\infty}}}{2\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}\,({\bm{w}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{K}}_{\sigma}{\bm{K}}{\bm{u}}_{i}).

Using the loss decomposition
P(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{M}p_{i}(t)+\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle L_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{M}s_{i}+\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}=\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{M}\frac{{\bm{w}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{K}}_{\sigma}{\bm{K}}{\bm{u}}_{i}}{\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}\Bigr)\sqrt{L_{\infty}}+\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2}\,\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(\operatorname{diag}({\bm{K}})^{1/2}{\bm{K}}_{\sigma}\bigr)\sqrt{L_{\infty}}+\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}=\frac{\gamma_{0}\pi}{4}\,\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(\operatorname{diag}({\bm{K}})^{1/2}\bigr)\sqrt{L_{\infty}}+\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}.

Solving the quadratic in
\sqrt{L_{\infty}}
gives

\displaystyle L_{\infty}=\left(\frac{\frac{\gamma_{0}\pi}{4}\,\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(\operatorname{diag}({\bm{K}})^{1/2}\bigr)+\sqrt{\left(\frac{\gamma_{0}\pi}{4}\,\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(\operatorname{diag}({\bm{K}})^{1/2}\bigr)\right)^{2}+4\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}}}{2}\right)^{2}\

(44)

\displaystyle\eqsim\ \max\Bigl\{\bigl(\gamma_{0}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{diag}({\bm{K}})^{1/2})\bigr)^{2},\ \lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}\Bigr\}.

(45)

Under our setup,

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{diag}({\bm{K}})^{1/2})=\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sqrt{({\bm{S}}{\bm{H}}{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}})_{ii}}\eqsim M\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{M}M^{\max(1-2\alpha,0)}}\eqsim M^{1-\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}.

By the results from
32
;
29
, and note in Appendix
K.3
,

\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}\ \eqsim\ M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}.

Hence

L_{\infty}\ \eqsim\ \max\!\Bigl\{\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M^{\,2-2\min(\alpha,\,0.5)},\ M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}\Bigr\}.

(46)

E.3.3
Proxy

Combining the early-phase decay (
43
) with the floor (
46
), we adopt

L_{\rm px}(N)\;:=\;\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N\bigr)^{-p}\;+\;\underbrace{\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M^{\,2-2\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}+M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}}_{=:C},\qquad p=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}.

(47)

E.3.4
Verification of the Proxy

We show that
L_{\rm px}
satisfies (
37
) up to absolute constants.
Equivalently, writing
Q_{L_{\rm px}}(N):=\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{L_{\rm px}(u)}}
, we establish

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q_{L_{\rm px}}(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}}_{\textbf{drift}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}}_{\textbf{approx}}\;+\;\underbrace{\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N}\!\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\frac{du}{\sqrt{L_{\rm px}(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz}_{\textbf{noise}}\

(48)

\displaystyle\eqsim\ \underbrace{\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N\bigr)^{-p}+C}_{L_{\rm px}(N)}.

(49)

Lower Bound

We prove

\displaystyle\textbf{drift}+\textbf{approx}+\textbf{noise}\;\gtrsim\;\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N\bigr)^{-p}+C.

(50)

Since
L_{\rm px}(u)\geq(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,u)^{-p}
,

drift

\displaystyle=\bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q_{L_{\rm px}}(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}\ \gtrsim\ \Bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\cdot\gamma_{0}\!\int_{0}^{N}\!\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,u\bigr)^{p/2}\,du\Bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}

\displaystyle\eqsim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N\bigr)^{-p}.

Since
L_{\rm px}(u)\geq C
for all
u
,

\int_{z}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{L_{\rm px}(u)}}\ \leq\ \frac{N-z}{\sqrt{C}}.

Hence

noise

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\frac{N-z}{\sqrt{C}}\Bigr)\,dz

(51)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\,\frac{\sqrt{C}}{\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}\Bigl(1-e^{-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\frac{N}{\sqrt{C}}}\Bigr)

(52)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\gamma_{0}\sqrt{C}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\frac{V_{i}}{\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}=\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2}\,\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(\operatorname{diag}(K)^{1/2}\bigr)\sqrt{C}\ \eqsim\ \gamma_{0}\,M^{1-\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,\sqrt{C}\ \gtrsim\ \gamma_{0}^{2}\,M^{\,2-2\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}.

(53)

Adding the approximation term
M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}
gives
\textbf{noise}+\textbf{approx}\gtrsim C
. Combining with the drift contribution yields (
50
).

Upper Bound

We establish

\displaystyle\textbf{drift}+\textbf{approx}+\textbf{noise}\;\lesssim\;\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N\bigr)^{-p}+C.

(54)

Let

A(N):=\max\Bigl\{(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N)^{-p},\ C\Bigr\},\qquad p=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}.

Then
L_{\rm px}(N)\eqsim A(N)
. Define
N_{0}
by
(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N_{0})^{-p}=C
, i.e.

A(N)=\begin{cases}(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N)^{-p},&N\leq N_{0},\\[2.0pt]
C,&N>N_{0}.\end{cases}

There exists a constant
B\geq 1
such that

L_{\rm px}(N)\ \leq\ B\,A(N)\qquad(\forall N\geq 0).

(55)

Upper bound for the drift term.

Since
L\leq BA
by (
73
) and
Q
is decreasing in its denominator,

\textbf{drift}=\bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q_{L}(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}\ \lesssim\ \bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,Q_{BA}(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}.

We evaluate the right-hand side by cases.

Case
N\leq N_{0}
.

Then
A(u)=(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,u)^{-p}
for
u\leq N
, so

Q_{BA}(N)=\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{BA(u)}}=\frac{c}{\sqrt{B}}\,\gamma_{0}\!\int_{0}^{N}\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,u\bigr)^{p/2}\,du

for an absolute constant
c>0
, which implies

\textbf{drift}\ \lesssim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}(1+p/2)}=\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N\bigr)^{-p}.

Case
N>N_{0}
.

Split the integral at
N_{0}
:

\displaystyle M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}Q_{BA}(N)

\displaystyle=\frac{c}{\sqrt{B}}\,\gamma_{0}M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\left[\int_{0}^{N_{0}}\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,u\bigr)^{p/2}\,du+\int_{N_{0}}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{BC}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{c}{\sqrt{B}}\left[\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N_{0}\bigr)^{1+p/2}+\gamma_{0}M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,\frac{N-N_{0}}{\sqrt{BC}}\right].

Raising to the power
-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}
and using
(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N_{0})^{-p}=C
,

drift

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[C^{-\,\frac{1+p/2}{p}}+\gamma_{0}\,\frac{N-N_{0}}{\sqrt{BC}}\right]^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}\ \leq\ \Bigl(C^{-\,\frac{1}{(2\alpha+2\beta-1)/(2\alpha)}}\Bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}=C.

Combining the two cases,

\textbf{drift}\ \lesssim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N\bigr)^{-p}+C.

(56)

Upper bound for the noise integral.

By the monotonicity of
r\mapsto r^{-1/2}
,

\int_{z}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\ \geq\ \frac{1}{\sqrt{B}}\int_{z}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{A(u)}}.

Therefore,

\textbf{noise}\ \leq\ \frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi\sqrt{B}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\frac{du}{\sqrt{A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz.

(57)

We again split into two cases.

Case
N\leq N_{0}
.

Then
A(u)=(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,u)^{-p}
on
[0,N]
, hence

\int_{z}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{A(u)}}=\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\bigr)^{p/2}\int_{z}^{N}u^{p/2}\,du=\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\bigr)^{p/2}\,\frac{N^{1+p/2}-z^{1+p/2}}{1+p/2}.

Plugging this into (
57
) and factoring,

noise

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi\sqrt{B}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\,\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\bigr)^{p/2}\,\frac{N^{1+p/2}-z^{1+p/2}}{1+p/2}\Bigr)\,dz

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\,\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi\sqrt{B}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\,\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\bigr)^{p/2}\,\frac{N^{1+p/2}}{1+p/2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\times\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi\sqrt{B}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\,\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\bigr)^{p/2}\,\frac{z^{1+p/2}}{1+p/2}\Bigr)\,dz.

Make the change of variables
y=z^{1+p/2}
so that

dz=\frac{1}{1+p/2}\,y^{\frac{1}{1+p/2}-1}\,dy
and the upper limit becomes
N^{1+p/2}
:

noise

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\,e^{-\alpha_{i}N^{1+p/2}}\int_{0}^{N^{1+p/2}}e^{\alpha_{i}y}\,\frac{1}{1+p/2}\,y^{\frac{1}{1+p/2}-1}\,dy,

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50157pt\alpha_{i}:=\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi\sqrt{B}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\,\frac{\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\bigr)^{p/2}}{1+p/2}.

Let
X:=N^{1+p/2}
and

g(y):=\frac{1}{1+p/2}\,y^{\frac{1}{1+p/2}-1}=\frac{1}{1+p/2}\,y^{-\,\frac{p}{2+p}}.

Since
e^{\alpha_{i}y}
is increasing and
g(y)
is decreasing on
(0,X]
,
Chebyshev’s integral inequality (oppositely monotone) yields

\frac{1}{X}\int_{0}^{X}e^{\alpha_{i}y}g(y)\,dy\ \leq\ \Bigl(\frac{1}{X}\int_{0}^{X}e^{\alpha_{i}y}\,dy\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{1}{X}\int_{0}^{X}g(y)\,dy\Bigr).

Hence

\displaystyle e^{-\alpha_{i}X}\!\int_{0}^{X}\!e^{\alpha_{i}y}g(y)\,dy

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\alpha_{i}X}\,\frac{e^{\alpha_{i}X}-1}{\alpha_{i}}\,\frac{1}{X}\!\int_{0}^{X}\!g(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1-e^{-\alpha_{i}X}}{\alpha_{i}}\,\frac{1}{1+p/2}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{p}{2+p}}\,X^{-\,\frac{p}{2+p}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-e^{-\alpha_{i}X}}{\alpha_{i}}\,X^{-\,\frac{p}{2+p}}\qquad\Bigl(\text{since }(1-\tfrac{p}{2+p})(1+\tfrac{p}{2})=1\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}}\,X^{-\,\frac{p}{2+p}}=\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}}\,N^{-p/2}.

Therefore

\textbf{noise}\ \leq\ \frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\,\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}}\,N^{-p/2},

and with

\alpha_{i}=\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi\sqrt{B}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\,\frac{\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\bigr)^{p/2}}{1+p/2}

this becomes

\textbf{noise}\ \leq\ \frac{\gamma_{0}\sqrt{B}}{2}\,(1+p/2)\,\sum_{i=1}^{M}\frac{V_{i}}{\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}\,\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N\bigr)^{-p/2}.

Using
\sum_{i}\frac{V_{i}}{\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}=\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{diag}({\bm{K}})^{1/2})\eqsim M^{1-\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}
, we get

noise

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{0}\,M^{1-\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N)^{-p/2}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{0}\,M^{1-\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N)^{p/2}\,(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N)^{-p}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{0}\,M^{1-\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{C}}\,(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N)^{-p}\ \lesssim\ (\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N)^{-p},

where we used
(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N)^{p/2}\leq(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N_{0})^{p/2}=C^{-1/2}
.

Case
N>N_{0}
.

Split the
z
–integral at
N_{0}
:

noise

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\left[\int_{0}^{N_{0}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi\sqrt{B}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N_{0}}\!\frac{du}{\sqrt{A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz+\int_{N_{0}}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi\sqrt{B}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\frac{du}{\sqrt{A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz\right].

The first integral is the
N=N_{0}
case just handled, hence

\int_{0}^{N_{0}}\cdots\,dz\ \lesssim\ (\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N_{0})^{-p}=C.

For the second integral, we use that
A\equiv C
on
[N_{0},N]
:

\displaystyle\int_{N_{0}}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi\sqrt{B}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\frac{du}{\sqrt{A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz

\displaystyle=\int_{N_{0}}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi\sqrt{B}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\,\frac{N-z}{\sqrt{C}}\Bigr)\,dz

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{C}}{\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi\sqrt{B}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}\left(1-e^{-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi\sqrt{B}}\,\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\,\frac{N-N_{0}}{\sqrt{C}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\pi\sqrt{B}}{4\gamma_{0}}\,\frac{\sqrt{C}}{\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}.

Therefore,

noise

\displaystyle\lesssim(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N_{0})^{-p}+\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\cdot\frac{\pi\sqrt{B}}{4\gamma_{0}}\,\frac{\sqrt{C}}{\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}

\displaystyle=C+\frac{\gamma_{0}\sqrt{B}}{2}\,\sqrt{C}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\frac{V_{i}}{\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}=C+\frac{\gamma_{0}\sqrt{B}}{2}\,\sqrt{C}\,\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{diag}({\bm{K}})^{1/2})

\displaystyle\lesssim C+\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,\sqrt{C}\ \lesssim\ C+\sqrt{C}\cdot\sqrt{C}\ \lesssim\ C.

Combining both cases,

\textbf{noise}\ \lesssim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N\bigr)^{-p}\ +\ C.

(58)

Conclusion of the upper bound.

From (
56
), (
58
), and
\textbf{approx}=M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}\leq C
, we obtain (
54
).

Finally, combining the lower bound (
50
) and the upper bound (
54
) proves (
49
). Therefore, the proxy (
67
) satisfies the implicit relation (
37
) up to absolute constants, with the three contributions labeled as
approx
,
drift
, and
noise
.

E.4
Constant Learning Rate: Proxy and Verification for the Case
\alpha>0.5
and
\beta>0.5
(Phase B)

We now handle the case
\alpha>0.5
and
\beta>0.5
. Since
\alpha>0.5
, we have
\min(\alpha,0.5)=0.5
, and because
\beta>0.5
, we have
\min(2\alpha,\,2\alpha+2\beta-1)=2\alpha
.
Applying the drift/approximation-term transformation to the ODE solution yields

\displaystyle L(N)\ \eqsim\ \underbrace{M^{-2\alpha}}_{\text{approx}}\;+\;\underbrace{\bigl(M^{1/2}Q(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{1}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-1}\bigl(M^{1/2}Q(N)\bigr)^{-1+\tfrac{1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{2}$}}\;

(59)

\displaystyle+\;\underbrace{\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\frac{du}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz}_{\text{noise}},

(60)

where

Q(N)=\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{L(u)}}.

E.4.1
Early Stage Proxies (drift
1
and drift
2
)

We extract proxies from the two drift terms in (
59
) by the same differentiate-and-separate trick as before.

drift
1
:
\bigl(M^{1/2}Q(N)\bigr)^{-(2\alpha+2\beta-1)/(2\alpha)}
.

Assuming this term dominates and replacing
\eqsim
by equality,

L(N)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha}{\,2\alpha+2\beta-1\,}}=M^{1/2}\gamma_{0}\!\int_{0}^{N}\!\frac{du}{\sqrt{L(u)}}.

Differentiation gives the separable ODE
L^{\prime}(t)=-\kappa_{1}\,L(t)^{\beta_{1}}
with

\beta_{1}=\frac{2\alpha}{\,2\alpha+2\beta-1\,}+\frac{1}{2},\qquad\kappa_{1}=\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}\,M^{1/2}\gamma_{0}.

For
\beta_{1}>1
(equivalently
2\alpha+2\beta<4\alpha+1
) we obtain

L_{1}(N)\ \eqsim\ (\gamma_{0}M^{1/2}N)^{-p_{1}},\qquad p_{1}=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}.

(61)

drift
2
:
M^{-1}\bigl(M^{1/2}Q(N)\bigr)^{-1+\tfrac{1}{2\alpha}}
.

Assume
\alpha>\tfrac{1}{2}
and, in the early phase, the second drift term dominates:

L(N)\ \eqsim\ M^{-1}\bigl(M^{1/2}Q(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha}},\qquad Q(N)\ \eqsim\ \gamma_{0}\int_{0}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{L(u)}}.

Expanding the
M
–exponent,

\bigl(M^{1/2}Q\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}=M^{\tfrac{-\,(2\alpha-1)}{4\alpha}}\,Q^{-\tfrac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha}},

hence

L(N)\ \eqsim\ M^{-\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha}}\;\bigl(\gamma_{0}I(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha}},\qquad I(N):=\int_{0}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{L(u)}}.

(62)

Raise both sides of (
62
) to the power
-\tfrac{2\alpha}{\,2\alpha-1\,}
so that the integral becomes linear:

L(N)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha}{\,2\alpha-1\,}}\;=\;M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{\,4\alpha-2\,}}\,\gamma_{0}\,I(N)\;\eqsim\;M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{\,4\alpha-2\,}}\,\gamma_{0}\int_{0}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{L(u)}}.

(63)

Differentiating (
63
) with respect to
t
yields

-\frac{2\alpha}{\,2\alpha-1\,}\,L(t)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha}{\,2\alpha-1\,}-1}\,L^{\prime}(t)=M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{\,4\alpha-2\,}}\,\gamma_{0}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{L(t)}}.

Rearranging gives a separable ODE of the usual power form

L^{\prime}(t)\;=\;-\,\kappa_{2}\,L(t)^{\beta_{2}},\qquad\beta_{2}\;=\;\frac{2\alpha}{\,2\alpha-1\,}+\frac{1}{2}\;=\;\frac{6\alpha-1}{\,4\alpha-2\,}\;>\;1,

(64)

with

\kappa_{2}\;=\;\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha}\,\gamma_{0}\,M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{\,4\alpha-2\,}}\;>\;0.

(65)

Since
\beta_{2}>1
, solving (
64
) gives

L(t)^{-(\beta_{2}-1)}\;=\;(\beta_{2}-1)\,\kappa_{2}\,t+\mathrm{const}.

Absorbing harmless absolute constants into
\eqsim
and setting
t=N
,

L_{2}(N)\ \eqsim\ \Bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{\,4\alpha-2\,}}\,N\Bigr)^{-p_{2}},\qquad p_{2}\;=\;\frac{1}{\beta_{2}-1}\;=\;\boxed{\ \frac{2(2\alpha-1)}{\,2\alpha+1\,}\ }.

(66)

Crossover scale.

Equating (
61
) and (
66
) gives

N_{1}\ \eqsim\ \gamma_{0}^{-1}\,M^{\eta},\qquad\eta=\frac{2\alpha+1-4\beta}{4\beta},

so
R_{1}
dominates for
N\lesssim N_{1}
and
L_{2}
for
N\gtrsim N_{1}
(when
\alpha>0.5
and
0.5<\beta<\alpha+0.5
).

E.4.2
Limit Stage (approx and noise floors)

As in the case
\alpha<0.5
or
\beta<0.5
, the stationary analysis with
f\equiv 1
yields

L_{\infty}\ \eqsim\ \max\!\bigl\{\gamma_{0}^{2}\,\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{diag}({\bm{K}})^{1/2})^{2},\ \lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}\bigr\}.

Under our standing model
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{diag}({\bm{K}})^{1/2})\eqsim M^{0.5}
and by the results from
32
;
29
, and note in Appendix
K.3
,
\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}\eqsim M^{-2\alpha}
, hence the floor

C\ :=\ \gamma_{0}^{2}\,M\;+\;M^{-2\alpha}.

E.4.3
Combined Proxy

\displaystyle L_{\rm px}(N)

\displaystyle:=\ L_{1}(N)+L_{2}(N)+C

(67)

\displaystyle=\ (\gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}N)^{-p_{1}}\;+\;\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}N\bigr)^{-p_{2}}\;+\;C,

where

p_{1}=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,},\qquad p_{2}=\frac{4\alpha-2}{\,2\alpha+1\,}.

E.4.4
Verification of the Proxy

We show that
L_{\rm px}
satisfies (
59
) up to absolute constants.

Lower bound.

We claim

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{L_{\rm px}}(N)\bigr)^{-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{1}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{L_{\rm px}}(N)\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{2}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-2\alpha}}_{\text{approx}}\;

(68)

\displaystyle+\;\underbrace{\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{L_{\rm px}(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz}_{\text{noise}}\ \gtrsim\ L_{\rm px}(N).

(69)

Drift part.
Using
L_{\rm px}\geq R_{1}
inside
Q
,

\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{L_{\rm px}}(N)\bigr)^{-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}\ \gtrsim\ \Bigl(M^{0.5}\gamma_{0}\int_{0}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{L_{1}(u)}}\Bigr)^{-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}\ \eqsim\ (\gamma_{0}M^{0.5}N)^{-p_{1}}\ \eqsim\ L_{1}(N).

Similarly, using
L_{\rm px}\geq L_{2}
inside
Q
,

M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{L_{\rm px}}(N)\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\ \gtrsim\ M^{-1}\Bigl(M^{0.5}\gamma_{0}\int_{0}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{L_{2}(u)}}\Bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\ \eqsim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}N\bigr)^{-p_{2}}\ \eqsim\ L_{2}(N).

Therefore,

\text{drift${}_{1}$}+\text{drift${}_{2}$}\ \gtrsim\ L_{1}(N)+L_{2}(N).

(70)

Noise
+
approx.
Since
L_{\rm px}\geq C
,

\int_{z}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{L_{\rm px}(u)}}\ \leq\ \frac{N-z}{\sqrt{C}}.

As in the Equation
53
,

\text{noise}\ \gtrsim\ \gamma_{0}\sqrt{C}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\frac{V_{i}}{\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}\ =\ \frac{\gamma_{0}}{2}\,\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(\operatorname{diag}(K)^{1/2}\bigr)\,\sqrt{C}\ \eqsim\ \gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}\sqrt{C}\ \gtrsim\ \gamma_{0}^{2}\,M.

Thus
\text{noise}+\text{approx}\gtrsim C
. Together with (
70
), this proves (
69
).

Upper bound.

We will prove

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{L_{\rm px}}(N)\bigr)^{-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{1}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{L_{\rm px}}(N)\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{2}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-2\alpha}}_{\text{approx}}\;

(71)

\displaystyle+\;\underbrace{\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N}\!\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{L_{\rm px}(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz}_{\text{noise}}\ \lesssim\ L_{\rm px}(N).

(72)

Let

A(N)=\begin{cases}(\gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}N)^{-p_{1}},&N\leq N_{1},\\[2.0pt]
\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}N\bigr)^{-p_{2}},&N_{1}\leq N\leq N_{2},\\[2.0pt]
C,&N>N_{2},\end{cases}

where
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
are the crossover points between the three terms.
There exists a constant
B\geq 1
such that

L_{\rm px}(N)\ \leq\ B\,A(N)\qquad(\forall N\geq 0).

(73)

It suffices to show

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\cdot A}(N)\bigr)^{-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{1}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\cdot A}(N)\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{2}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-2\alpha}}_{\text{approx}}\;

(74)

\displaystyle+\;\underbrace{\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz}_{\text{noise}}\ \lesssim\ L_{\rm px}(N).

(75)

Case
N\leq N_{1}
.

It is enough to prove

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\cdot A}(N)\bigr)^{-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{1}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\cdot A}(N)\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{2}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-2\alpha}}_{\text{approx}}\;

(76)

\displaystyle+\;\underbrace{\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz}_{\text{noise}}\ \lesssim\ (\gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}N)^{-p_{1}}.

(77)

We have
M^{-2\alpha}\lesssim(\gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}N)^{-p_{1}}
directly.
Also, the following holds with straightforward integration.

\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\cdot A}(N)\bigr)^{-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}\eqsim(\gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}N)^{-p_{1}}.

Since
N\leq N_{1}\eqsim\gamma_{0}^{-1}\,M^{\eta}
with
\eta=\tfrac{2\alpha+1-4\beta}{4\beta}
, following holds by integration and calculation.

M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\cdot A}(N)\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\ \lesssim\ (\gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}N)^{-p_{1}}.

Finally, arguing as in the
N\leq N_{0}
case of Section
E.3.4
,

\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz\ \lesssim\ (\gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}N)^{-p_{1}}.

Hence, the claim holds for
N\leq N_{1}
.

Case
N_{1}\leq N\leq N_{2}
.

We will show

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N)\bigr)^{-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{1}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N)\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{2}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-2\alpha}}_{\text{approx}}\;

(78)

\displaystyle+\;\underbrace{\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N}\!\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\!\cdot\!A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz}_{\text{noise}}\ \lesssim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}\,N\bigr)^{-p_{2}},

(79)

where

p_{1}=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,},\qquad p_{2}=\frac{2(2\alpha-1)}{\,2\alpha+1\,},\qquad A(u)=\begin{cases}(\gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}u)^{-p_{1}},&u\leq N_{1},\\[2.0pt]
\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}u\bigr)^{-p_{2}},&N_{1}<u\leq N,\\
\end{cases}

and
Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N)=\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{N}\frac{du}{\sqrt{B\!\cdot\!A(u)}}
.

Approx term.

Since
N\leq N_{2}
,

M^{-2\alpha}\ \lesssim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}\,N\bigr)^{-p_{2}}.

Drift term.

If
N_{1}\leq N\leq 2N_{1}
, using the case
N\leq N_{1}
, we get an inequality for two drift terms.

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N)\bigr)^{-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{1}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N)\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{2}$}}

(80)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N_{1})\bigr)^{-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{1}$}}\;+\;\underbrace{M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N_{1})\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}}_{\text{drift${}_{2}$}}

(81)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}N_{1})^{-p_{1}}\lesssim(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}\,N\bigr)^{-p_{2}}.

(82)

So while covering the drift term, we will temporarily assume
2N_{1}\leq N
.

Lower bound on
Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N)
.

Split the integral at
N_{1}
:

\displaystyle Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N)

\displaystyle\eqsim\gamma_{0}\!\int_{0}^{N_{1}}\!\frac{du}{\sqrt{A(u)}}+\gamma_{0}\!\int_{N_{1}}^{N}\!\frac{du}{\sqrt{A(u)}}=:\gamma_{0}\,(I_{1}+I_{2}).

(83)

For the first part, using
A(u)=(\gamma_{0}M^{0.5}u)^{-p_{1}}
on
[0,N_{1}]
,

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=(\gamma_{0}M^{0.5})^{p_{1}/2}\int_{0}^{N_{1}}u^{p_{1}/2}\,du=\frac{(\gamma_{0}M^{0.5})^{p_{1}/2}}{1+p_{1}/2}\,N_{1}^{1+p_{1}/2}.

(84)

For the second part, using
A(u)=(\gamma_{0}M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}u)^{-p_{2}}
on
[N_{1},N]
,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=(\gamma_{0}M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}})^{p_{2}/2}\int_{N_{1}}^{N}u^{p_{2}/2}\,du=\frac{(\gamma_{0}M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}})^{p_{2}/2}}{1+p_{2}/2}\,\bigl(N^{1+p_{2}/2}-N_{1}^{1+p_{2}/2}\bigr).

(85)

Since we temporarily assumed
N\geq 2N_{1}
, we have

I_{2}\gtrsim(\gamma_{0}M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}})^{p_{2}/2}N^{1+p_{2}/2}.

Hence, from (
83
),

Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N)\ \gtrsim\ \gamma_{0}\,(\gamma_{0}M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}})^{p_{2}/2}\,N^{1+p_{2}/2}.

(86)

drift
1
vs. drift
2
.

From
N\geq N_{1}
and (
86
), we have

Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N)\geq Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N_{1})
.
It follows that

\text{drift${}_{1}$}=\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N)\bigr)^{-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}\ \leq\ M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N)\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}=\text{drift${}_{2}$},

so it suffices to control drift
2
.

drift
2
bound.

Using (
86
),

drift
2

\displaystyle=M^{-1}\Bigl(M^{0.5}\,Q_{B\!\cdot\!A}(N)\Bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{-1}\Bigl(M^{0.5}\cdot\gamma_{0}^{\,1+p_{2}/2}\,M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}\cdot\frac{p_{2}}{2}}\,N^{1+p_{2}/2}\Bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}.

(87)

Now compute the exponents of
N
,
\gamma_{0}
, and
M
separately.

(i)
N
–exponent:

\Bigl(1+\tfrac{p_{2}}{2}\Bigr)\Bigl(-1+\tfrac{1}{2\alpha}\Bigr)=\Bigl(1+\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha+1}\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{1}{2\alpha}-1\Bigr)=\frac{4\alpha}{2\alpha+1}\cdot\Bigl(-\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha}\Bigr)=-\,\frac{2(2\alpha-1)}{2\alpha+1}=-\,p_{2}.

(ii)
\gamma_{0}
–exponent:
the same calculation as in (i) gives
-p_{2}
.

(iii)
M
–exponent:
the total exponent equals

-1\;+\;\Bigl(-1+\tfrac{1}{2\alpha}\Bigr)\!\left(0.5\;+\;\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}\cdot\frac{p_{2}}{2}\right).

A direct simplification shows this equals

-\,\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}\,p_{2}
.
Therefore, from (
87
),

\text{drift${}_{2}$}\ \lesssim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}\,N\bigr)^{-p_{2}}.

(88)

Since
\text{drift}_{1}\leq\text{drift}_{2}
, we also have
\text{drift}_{1}\lesssim(\gamma_{0}M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}N)^{-p_{2}}
.

Noise bound.

It suffices to show

\displaystyle\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N_{1}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz\;

(89)

\displaystyle+\;\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{N_{1}}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz\ \lesssim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}\,N\bigr)^{-p_{2}}.

(90)

Integral over
[N_{1},N]
.

As in the case
N\leq N_{0}
of Section
E.3.4
, with
A(u)=(\gamma_{0}M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}u)^{-p_{2}}
on
[N_{1},N]
,

\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{N_{1}}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz\ \lesssim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}\,N\bigr)^{-p_{2}}.

Integral over
[0,N_{1}]
.

First,

\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N_{1}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz\;\leq\;\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N_{1}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N_{1}}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz.

As in the case
N\leq N_{0}
of Section
E.3.4
,

\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N_{1}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N_{1}}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz\ \lesssim\ \sqrt{C}\,\bigl(\gamma_{0}M^{0.5}N\bigr)^{-p_{1}/2}\ \lesssim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}\,N_{1}\bigr)^{-p_{2}}.

If
N\leq 2N_{1}
, this already implies

\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N_{1}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz\ \lesssim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}\,N\bigr)^{-p_{2}}.

If
N>2N_{1}
, then

\int_{0}^{N_{1}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz\ \leq\ N_{1}\,\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{N_{1}}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr),

and, using
e^{-x}\leq 1/x
together with the lower bound

\int_{N_{1}}^{N}\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\gtrsim N^{\,1+p_{2}/2}\,(\gamma_{0}M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}})^{p_{2}/2}
,
we get

\displaystyle\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N_{1}}\!\cdots\,dz

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\,\frac{N_{1}}{\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{N_{1}}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{0}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\frac{V_{i}}{\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})}\,\bigl(\gamma_{0}M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}N\bigr)^{-p_{2}/2}\

\displaystyle\eqsim\ \gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}\,\bigl(\gamma_{0}M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}N\bigr)^{-p_{2}/2}\lesssim\bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}\,N\bigr)^{-p_{2}},

where the last step uses

\gamma_{0}M^{0.5}\leq(\gamma_{0}M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}N)^{-p_{2}/2}
which holds from
N\leq N_{2}
.

Combining the
[N_{1},N]
and
[0,N_{1}]
bounds yields

\text{noise}\ \lesssim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}\,N\bigr)^{-p_{2}},

as required for the case
N_{1}\leq N\leq N_{2}
.

Case
N\geq N_{2}
.

We have
M^{-2\alpha}\lesssim C
directly. As in the above case,

\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\cdot A}(N)\bigr)^{-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}\leq M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\cdot A}(N)\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}.

Using the estimate from the previous case,

M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\cdot A}(N)\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\ \lesssim\ M^{-1}\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{B\cdot A}(N_{2})\bigr)^{-1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\ \lesssim\ \bigl(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{4\alpha-2}}N_{2}\bigr)^{-p_{2}}\ \lesssim\ C.

Finally, as in the
N>N_{0}
case of Section
E.3.4
,

\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\!\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{K})\!\int_{z}^{N}\!\tfrac{du}{\sqrt{B\cdot A(u)}}\Bigr)\,dz\ \lesssim\ C.

Therefore, the bound holds for
N\geq N_{2}
as well.

E.5
Note on the Regime
\beta>\alpha+0.5

When
\beta>\alpha+0.5
, the assumption
\zeta>1
used in step
42
no longer holds.
In this case, the first drift term takes a different form:

L_{\text{drift}_{1}}(N)\ \eqsim\ \Bigl(1-\kappa\,\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,0.5)}\,N\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\beta-2\alpha-1\,}},

for a finite horizon and some constant
\kappa
.
Inserting the max function, we can represent it as a global function.

L_{\text{drift}_{1}}(N)\ \eqsim\ \Bigl(\max\left(1-\kappa\,\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,0.5)}\,N,0\right)\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\beta-2\alpha-1\,}}.

Now we explain the behavior of the term.
When
N
is asymptotically smaller than
(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,0.5)})^{-1}
, the term is asymptotically constant.
On
N\eqsim(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,0.5)})^{-1}
, the term suddenly drops from a constant scale to
0
.

For the case
\alpha<0.5
or
\beta<0.5
the valid proxy is

L_{\rm px}(N):=\Bigl(\max\left(1-\kappa\,\gamma_{0}\,M^{\min(\alpha,0.5)}\,N,0\right)\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\beta-2\alpha-1\,}}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M^{\,2-2\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}+M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)},

and for the case
\alpha>0.5
and
\beta>0.5
the valid proxy is

L_{\rm px}(N):=\Bigl(\max\left(1-\kappa\,\gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}\,N,0\right)\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\beta-2\alpha-1\,}}+M^{-\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{2\alpha+1}}(N\gamma_{0})^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha-1)}{2\alpha+1}}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M+M^{-2\alpha}.

These satisfy the implicit integral equation, same as Sections
E.3.4
and
E.4.4
.

Therefore, for the case
\alpha<0.5
,
\beta>\alpha+0.5
,

R(M,N,\gamma_{0})=\Bigl(\max\left(1-\kappa\,\gamma_{0}\,M^{\alpha}\,N,0\right)\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\beta-2\alpha-1\,}}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M^{\,2-2\alpha}+M^{-2\alpha},

(91)

and for the case
\alpha>0.5
and
\beta>0.5
,

R(M,N,\gamma_{0})=\Bigl(\max\left(1-\kappa\,\gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}\,N,0\right)\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\beta-2\alpha-1\,}}+M^{-\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{2\alpha+1}}(N\gamma_{0})^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha-1)}{2\alpha+1}}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M+M^{-2\alpha}.

(92)

Appendix F
Derivation of the Compute-Optimal Result

Goal.

The main goal of this section is to derive compute-optimal scaling laws of signSGD in the following form:

M^{\star}\eqsim\mathfrak{f}^{\xi},\qquad R\left(M^{\star},\tfrac{\mathfrak{f}}{M^{\star}},\gamma_{0}^{\star}\right)\eqsim\mathfrak{f}^{-\eta}.

Here
R(M,N,\gamma_{0})
denote the
L({\bm{\theta}}_{N})
under learning rate
\gamma_{0}
and fixed model size
M
.
We define the computational budget in terms of FLOPS as
\mathfrak{f}=MN
, and consider the optimal model size
M^{\star}
under fixed
\mathfrak{f}
, and optimal scaling of learning rate in the form
\gamma_{0}^{\star}=M^{-e^{*}}
.

Proof Overview.

Substituting the learning rate
\gamma_{0}=M^{-e}
into our loss formula

\displaystyle R(M,N,\gamma_{0})\;\eqsim\;M^{-2\alpha+\max(0,\,1-2\beta)}+\bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,0.5)}N\gamma_{0}\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{2\alpha-2\beta+1}}

\displaystyle+M^{-\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{2\alpha+1}}(N\gamma_{0})^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha-1)}{2\alpha+1}}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M^{2-\min(1,2\alpha)},

we can represent the risk as a function of three variables
M
,
N
,
e
, and two parameters
\alpha
,
\beta
.

Then for fixed compute
\mathfrak{f}=MN
, we substitute
M=\mathfrak{f}^{x}
and
N=\mathfrak{f}^{1-x}
to express the risk as the function of three variables
\mathfrak{f}
,
x
,
e
and two parameters
\alpha
,
\beta
.
Four terms in the loss formula convert to four terms with exponential of FLOPS
\mathfrak{f}
with exponent functions
\ell_{1}
to
\ell_{4}
.

R(\mathfrak{f},x,e,\alpha,\beta)\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{-\ell_{1}(x,e,\alpha,\beta)}+\mathfrak{f}^{-\ell_{2}(x,e,\alpha,\beta)}+\mathfrak{f}^{-\ell_{3}(x,e,\alpha,\beta)}+\mathfrak{f}^{-\ell_{4}(x,e,\alpha,\beta)}.

Since each term is a power of
\mathfrak{f}
, and assuming
\mathfrak{f}\geq 1
, the loss simplifies to

R(\mathfrak{f},x,e)\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{-h(x,e,\alpha,\beta)},\quad\text{where}\ \ h(x,e,\alpha,\beta)=\min(\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3},\ell_{4}).

We find the optimal learning rate exponent
e^{*}
and the optimal model size exponent by

x^{*},e^{*}=\argmax_{x,e}h(x,e,\alpha,\beta).

As we optimize over two variables
x
and
e
, three terms among
\ell_{1}
to
\ell_{4}
balance on the optimal values
x^{*}
and
e^{*}
.

Then the optimal learning rate is
\gamma_{0}^{*}=M^{-e^{*}}
, and the optimal model size is
M^{\star}=\mathfrak{f}^{x^{*}}
.
Finally, the compute-optimal scaling law is

R(M^{\star},\mathfrak{f}/M^{\star},\gamma_{0}^{*})\;=\;\mathfrak{f}^{-h(x^{*},e^{*},\alpha,\beta)},

and
h(x^{*},e^{*},\alpha,\beta)
will be the compute-optimal slope in absolute value.

F.1
Compute-Optimal Result for Maximal Learning Rate

We now discuss the maximal learning rate case deferred from the main text.
Note that
32
showed that the maximal learning rate for SGD is
\gamma_{0}\eqsim 1
when
\alpha>\tfrac{1}{2}
, and
\gamma_{0}\eqsim M^{-(1-2\alpha)}
when
\alpha<\tfrac{1}{2}
.

Now, we discuss the maximal learning rate for signSGD.
Because the noise term is
\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M^{\,2-\min(1,\,2\alpha)}
, stability requires

\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M^{\,2-\min(1,\,2\alpha)}\;\lesssim\;1.

Otherwise, the signSGD noise term explodes as
M
grows.
This condition is satisfied by choosing

\gamma_{0}\;=\;M^{-1+\min(\alpha,\,0.5)},

which ensures
\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M^{\,2-\min(1,\,2\alpha)}\eqsim 1
while the other terms still decay appropriately.

For
\alpha<0.5
, the term

\bigl(M^{\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}\,N\,\gamma_{0}\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha-2\beta+1\,}}\;=\;\bigl(M^{-(1-2\alpha)}\,N\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha-2\beta+1\,}}

decreases with
N
but increases with
M
.
However, under a fixed compute budget
\mathfrak{f}=MN
, one can allocate resources so that this term does not cause an exploding loss; hence we do not classify it as unstable.

Thus, the maximal learning rate for signSGD is

\gamma_{0}\;=\;M^{-1+\min(\alpha,\,0.5)}.

In this case, however, we obtain
R(M,N,\gamma_{0})\eqsim 1
, so the slope of the compute-optimal curve is always zero.

F.2
Derivation of Compute-Optimal Result for Optimal Learning Rate

We assume
\alpha+\beta>0.5
throughout, even for the case where it is not specified.

F.2.1
\alpha>0.5,\;\beta<0.5
(Phase A
a
)

We start from

R(M,N,\gamma_{0})\eqsim\bigl(M^{1/2}N\gamma_{0}\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}}+M^{-(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M.

Substitute

\gamma_{0}=M^{-e},\qquad N=\frac{\mathfrak{f}}{M},\qquad M=\mathfrak{f}^{x},

so that, up to constant factors,

R\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{\,\max\{\ell_{1}(x),\,\ell_{2}(x),\,\ell_{3}(x)\}},

where

\displaystyle\ell_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=-\,(2\alpha+2\beta-1)\,x,

\displaystyle\ell_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}\,\Bigl(e+\tfrac{1}{2}\Bigr)\,x\;-\;\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,},

\displaystyle\ell_{3}(x)

\displaystyle=(1-2e)\,x.

We minimize the convex, piecewise–linear function
f(x,e)=\max_{i}\ell_{i}(x,e)
over
x\in(0,1)
and
e\in\mathbb{R}
.
By convexity, any interior minimizer must occur at a kink where at least two lines are active.
In our regime
\alpha+\beta>0.5
and
\beta<\alpha+0.5
, the only admissible triple intersection is
\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3}\}
.
Solving
\ell_{1}=\ell_{3}
and
\ell_{2}=\ell_{3}
yields

e^{*}=\alpha+\beta,\qquad x^{*}=\frac{1}{\,2\alpha+1\,},\qquad h^{*}=\ell_{1}(x^{*})=\ell_{2}(x^{*})=\ell_{3}(x^{*})=-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{\,2\alpha+1\,}.

To verify that this kink is the global minimizer, note first that
x^{*}\in(0,1)
when
\alpha>0.5
, hence it is interior.
Next, the subgradient optimality condition for convex max-of-lines problems requires
(0,0)\in\partial f(x^{*},e^{*})
.
At
(x^{*},e^{*})
the active lines have slopes that straddle zero in both coordinates:

\partial_{x}\ell_{1}=-(2\alpha+2\beta-1)<0,\quad\partial_{x}\ell_{2}=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}\Bigl(e^{*}+\tfrac{1}{2}\Bigr)>0,\quad\partial_{x}\ell_{3}=1-2e^{*}=1-2(\alpha+\beta)<0,

and

\partial_{e}\ell_{1}=0,\qquad\partial_{e}\ell_{2}=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}\,x^{*}>0,\qquad\partial_{e}\ell_{3}=-2x^{*}<0.

Since
0
lies in the convex hull of the active slopes in both
x
and
e
, we have
(0,0)\in\partial f(x^{*},e^{*})
, so the interior triple intersection is the global minimizer; no boundary check is needed.

\boxed{\gamma_{0}=M^{-(\alpha+\beta)},\quad M^{\star}\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{1/(2\alpha+1)},\quad R\!\left(M^{\star},\tfrac{\mathfrak{f}}{M^{\star}}\right)\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{\,2\alpha+1\,}}.}

F.2.2
\alpha<0.5,\;\beta<0.5
(Phase A
b
)

We start from

R(M,N,\gamma_{0})=\bigl(M^{\alpha}N\gamma_{0}\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}}+M^{-(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M^{\,2-2\alpha}.

Substitute

\gamma_{0}=M^{-e},\qquad N=\frac{\mathfrak{f}}{M},\qquad M=\mathfrak{f}^{x},

so that, up to constant factors,

R\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{\,\max\{\ell_{1}(x),\,\ell_{2}(x),\,\ell_{3}(x)\}},

where

\displaystyle\ell_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=-\,(2\alpha+2\beta-1)\,x,

\displaystyle\ell_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}\,\bigl(\alpha-e-1\bigr)\,x\;-\;\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,},

\displaystyle\ell_{3}(x)

\displaystyle=\bigl(2-2\alpha-2e\bigr)\,x.

We minimize the convex, piecewise–linear function
f(x,e)=\max_{i}\ell_{i}(x,e)
over
x\in(0,1)
and
e\in\mathbb{R}
.
Under our standing assumptions
\alpha+\beta>0.5
and
\beta<\alpha+0.5
, the only admissible triple intersection is
\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3}\}
.
Solving
\ell_{1}=\ell_{3}
and
\ell_{2}=\ell_{3}
gives

e^{*}=\beta+\tfrac{1}{2},\qquad x^{*}=\tfrac{1}{2},\qquad h^{*}=\ell_{1}(x^{*})=\ell_{2}(x^{*})=\ell_{3}(x^{*})=-\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2}.

To certify optimality, note that
x^{*}\in(0,1)
(since
x^{*}=\tfrac{1}{2}
) and check the subgradient condition
(0,0)\in\partial f(x^{*},e^{*})
.
At
(x^{*},e^{*})
the active lines have slopes straddling zero in both coordinates:

\partial_{x}\ell_{1}=-(2\alpha+2\beta-1)<0,\quad\partial_{x}\ell_{2}=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}\bigl(e^{*}+1-\alpha\bigr)>0,\quad\partial_{x}\ell_{3}=2-2\alpha-2e^{*}=1-2(\alpha+\beta)<0,

and

\partial_{e}\ell_{1}=0,\qquad\partial_{e}\ell_{2}=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}\,x^{*}>0,\qquad\partial_{e}\ell_{3}=-2x^{*}<0.

Hence
0
lies in the convex hull of the active slopes in both variables, so the interior kink
(x^{*},e^{*})
is the global minimizer; no boundary check is required.

\boxed{\gamma_{0}=M^{-(\beta+0.5)},\quad M^{\star}\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{1/2},\quad R\!\left(M^{\star},\tfrac{\mathfrak{f}}{M^{\star}}\right)\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2}}.}

F.2.3
\alpha<0.5,\;0.5<\beta<\alpha+0.5
(Phase A
c
)

We start from

R(M,N,\gamma_{0})=\bigl(M^{\alpha}N\gamma_{0}\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}}+M^{-2\alpha}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M^{\,2-2\alpha}.

Substitute

\gamma_{0}=M^{-e},\qquad N=\frac{\mathfrak{f}}{M},\qquad M=\mathfrak{f}^{x},

so that, up to constant factors,

R\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{\,\max\{\ell_{1}(x),\,\ell_{2}(x),\,\ell_{3}(x)\}},

where

\displaystyle\ell_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=-\,2\alpha\,x,

\displaystyle\ell_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}\,\Bigl(\alpha-e-1\Bigr)\,x\;-\;\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,},

\displaystyle\ell_{3}(x)

\displaystyle=\bigl(2-2\alpha-2e\bigr)\,x.

We minimize the convex, piecewise–linear objective
f(x,e)=\max_{i}\ell_{i}(x,e)
over
x\in(0,1)
and
e\in\mathbb{R}
.
In the regime
\alpha+\beta>0.5
and
\beta<\alpha+0.5
(with
\alpha<0.5<\beta
), the only admissible triple intersection is
\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3}\}
.
Solving
\ell_{1}=\ell_{3}
and
\ell_{2}=\ell_{3}
yields

e^{*}=1,\qquad x^{*}=\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{\,-4\alpha\beta+6\alpha+4\beta-2\,},\qquad h^{*}=\ell_{1}(x^{*})=\ell_{2}(x^{*})=\ell_{3}(x^{*})=-\,\frac{2\alpha\,(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,-4\alpha\beta+6\alpha+4\beta-2\,}.

One checks that the denominator is positive in this regime and exceeds the positive numerator
2\alpha+2\beta-1
, hence
x^{*}\in(0,1)
.

Interior optimality.

At
(x^{*},e^{*})
the active lines’ slopes straddle zero in both coordinates:

\partial_{x}\ell_{1}=-2\alpha<0,\quad\partial_{x}\ell_{2}=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}\,(e^{*}+1-\alpha)>0,\quad\partial_{x}\ell_{3}=2-2\alpha-2e^{*}=-2\alpha<0,

and

\partial_{e}\ell_{1}=0,\qquad\partial_{e}\ell_{2}=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}\,x^{*}>0,\qquad\partial_{e}\ell_{3}=-2x^{*}<0.

Thus
(0,0)\in\partial f(x^{*},e^{*})
and, with
x^{*}\in(0,1)
, the interior kink is the global minimizer; no boundary check is required.

\boxed{\gamma_{0}=M^{-1},\quad M^{\star}\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{\,-4\alpha\beta+6\alpha+4\beta-2\,}},\quad R\!\bigl(M^{\star},\tfrac{\mathfrak{f}}{M^{\star}}\bigr)\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{-\frac{2\alpha\,(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,-4\alpha\beta+6\alpha+4\beta-2\,}}.}

F.2.4
\alpha>0.5,\;0.5<\beta<\alpha+0.5
(Phase B
a
)

We start from

R(M,N,\gamma_{0})=\bigl(M^{1/2}N\gamma_{0}\bigr)^{-\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}}+\bigl(M^{\frac{6\alpha-1}{\,4\alpha-2\,}}N\gamma_{0}\bigr)^{-\frac{2(2\alpha-1)}{\,2\alpha+1\,}}+M^{-2\alpha}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M.

Substitute
\gamma_{0}=M^{-e},\ N=\mathfrak{f}/M,\ M=\mathfrak{f}^{x}
. Then, up to
\mathfrak{f}
-independent factors,

R\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{\,\max_{i=1,\dots,4}\ell_{i}(x,e)},

where

\displaystyle\ell_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=-\,2\alpha\,x,

\displaystyle\ell_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}\,\Bigl(e+\tfrac{1}{2}\Bigr)x\;-\;\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,},

\displaystyle\ell_{3}(x)

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\frac{2(2\alpha-1)}{\,2\alpha+1\,}\,e-1\Bigr)x\;-\;\frac{2(2\alpha-1)}{\,2\alpha+1\,},

\displaystyle\ell_{4}(x)

\displaystyle=(1-2e)\,x.

We minimize the convex, piecewise–linear function
f(x,e)=\max_{i}\ell_{i}(x,e)
over
x\in(0,1)
,
e\in\mathbb{R}
.
In the regime
\alpha>0.5,\ \beta>0.5
, the only admissible interior kink with three active lines is
\{\ell_{2},\ell_{3},\ell_{4}\}
.
Solving
\ell_{2}=\ell_{4}
and
\ell_{3}=\ell_{4}
yields

e^{*}=\frac{2\alpha+4\beta-1}{\,4\beta\,},\qquad x^{*}=\frac{\beta}{\,\alpha+\beta\,},\qquad h^{*}=\ell_{2}(x^{*},e^{*})=\ell_{3}(x^{*},e^{*})=\ell_{4}(x^{*},e^{*})=-\,\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{\,2\alpha+2\beta\,}.

Interior optimality.

First,
x^{*}\in(0,1)
since
\alpha,\beta>0.5
.
Second, the subgradient condition
(0,0)\in\partial f(x^{*},e^{*})
holds because the active slopes straddle zero in both variables:

\partial_{x}\ell_{2}=\tfrac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}\Bigl(e^{*}+\tfrac{1}{2}\Bigr)>0,\quad\partial_{x}\ell_{3}=\tfrac{2(2\alpha-1)}{\,2\alpha+1\,}e^{*}-1<0,\quad\partial_{x}\ell_{4}=1-2e^{*}=\tfrac{1-2\alpha-2\beta}{\,2\beta\,}<0,

and

\partial_{e}\ell_{2}=\tfrac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}\,x^{*}>0,\qquad\partial_{e}\ell_{3}=\tfrac{2(2\alpha-1)}{\,2\alpha+1\,}\,x^{*}>0,\qquad\partial_{e}\ell_{4}=-2x^{*}<0.

Hence
(x^{*},e^{*})
is the global minimizer among interior points. It remains to exclude
\ell_{1}
at
(x^{*},e^{*})
:

\ell_{1}(x^{*})=-2\alpha\,\frac{\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\ \leq\ -\frac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{\,2(\alpha+\beta)\,}=h^{*},

since
4\alpha\beta-2\alpha-2\beta+1=4(\alpha-\tfrac{1}{2})(\beta-\tfrac{1}{2})\geq 0
for
\alpha,\beta>0.5
.
Therefore the triple intersection
\{\ell_{2},\ell_{3},\ell_{4}\}
is the global optimum.

\boxed{\gamma_{0}=M^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+4\beta-1}{\,4\beta\,}},\quad M^{\star}\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{\tfrac{\beta}{\,\alpha+\beta\,}},\quad R\!\left(M^{\star},\tfrac{\mathfrak{f}}{M^{\star}}\right)\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{\,2\alpha+2\beta\,}}.}

F.2.5
\alpha<0.5,\;\beta>\alpha+0.5
(Phase A
d
)

Recall the loss formula (
91
)

R(M,N,\gamma_{0})=\Bigl(\max\left(1-\kappa\,\gamma_{0}\,M^{\alpha}\,N,0\right)\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\beta-2\alpha-1\,}}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M^{\,2-2\alpha}+M^{-2\alpha}.

Note that the drift term vanishes at
N\eqsim(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\alpha})^{-1}
.

Let
\gamma_{0}=M^{-e}
.
Note that because of the approximation error
M^{-2\alpha}
, there is no gain from setting
e
bigger than
1
.
So we will only consider the case
e\leq 1
.
In that case, loss is a constant scale before
N\eqsim M^{e-\alpha}
, and it drops to the scale of
M^{-2e-2\alpha+2}
.

Since a constant scale loss cannot be compute-optimal, the loss
M^{-2e-2\alpha+2}
at
N\eqsim M^{e-\alpha}
will be a candidate for the compute-optimal point.
In that case
\mathfrak{f}=MN=M^{1+e-\alpha}
holds and it leads to
M=\mathfrak{f}^{\frac{1}{1+e-\alpha}}
.
So the loss
M^{-2e-2\alpha+2}
has the size
\mathfrak{f}^{\frac{-2e-2\alpha+2}{1+e-\alpha}}
.

Since
e=1
minimizes
\frac{-2e-2\alpha+2}{1+e-\alpha}
,
\gamma_{0}=M^{-1}
is the optimal learning rate.
This leads to the following result.

\boxed{\gamma_{0}=M^{-1},\quad M^{\star}\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{\tfrac{1}{2-\alpha}},\quad R\!\left(M^{\star},\tfrac{\mathfrak{f}}{M^{\star}}\right)\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{-\tfrac{2\alpha}{2-\alpha}}.}

F.2.6
\alpha>0.5,\;\beta>\alpha+0.5
(Phase B
b
)

Recall the loss formula (
92
)

R(M,N,\gamma_{0})=\Bigl(\max\left(1-\kappa\,\gamma_{0}\,M^{0.5}\,N,0\right)\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\beta-2\alpha-1\,}}+M^{-\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{2\alpha+1}}(N\gamma_{0})^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha-1)}{2\alpha+1}}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M+M^{-2\alpha}.

Note that the first term vanishes at
N\eqsim(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\alpha})^{-1}
.
At that point second term becomes
M^{-\tfrac{6\alpha-1}{2\alpha+1}}(N\gamma_{0})^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha-1)}{2\alpha+1}}\eqsim M^{-\frac{4\alpha}{2\alpha+1}}
.

As we optimize over three parameters
N
,
M
,
\gamma_{0}
, and one constraint
\mathfrak{f}=MN
, we have two degrees of freedom.
So this means three terms may balance together at the compute-optimal point.

The first possible case is the balance of the first three terms, and in this case,
\gamma_{0}^{2}M=M^{-\frac{4\alpha}{2\alpha+1}}
and
N\eqsim(\gamma_{0}\,M^{\alpha})^{-1}
must hold.
Here, the loss is
M^{-\frac{4\alpha}{2\alpha+1}}
and
\mathfrak{f}=MN=M^{\frac{2\alpha+1}{4\alpha+1}}
holds, so the loss is
\mathfrak{f}^{-\frac{4\alpha}{4\alpha+1}}
.

The second possible case is the balance of the last three terms, and after solving the equations, the loss is
\mathfrak{f}^{-\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+1}}
.

The first case has a steeper decay, so it is the compute-optimal.
This leads to the following result.

\boxed{\gamma_{0}=M^{-\tfrac{6\alpha+1}{4\alpha+2}},\quad M^{\star}\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{\tfrac{2\alpha+1}{4\alpha+1}},\quad R\!\left(M^{\star},\tfrac{\mathfrak{f}}{M^{\star}}\right)\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{-\tfrac{4\alpha}{4\alpha+1}}.}

F.3
Discussion for the Suboptimal Learning Rate

In this section, we calculate the compute-optimal exponent for a general size of learning rate in the form of
\gamma_{0}=M^{-e}
.
We will focus on Phase Aa.
In that phase, the maximal learning rate was
\gamma_{0}=M^{-1/2}
and optimal learning rate was
\gamma_{0}^{\star}=M^{-(\alpha+\beta)}
.

In this section, we will calculate the compute-optimal exponent for general
e\geq 1/2
.

Recall that we have the following loss formula for Phase Aa.

R(M,N,\gamma_{0})\eqsim\bigl(M^{1/2}N\gamma_{0}\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}}+M^{-(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M.

For the case
1/2\leq e\leq(\alpha+\beta)
,
\bigl(M^{1/2}N\gamma_{0}\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}}
and
\gamma_{0}^{2}\,M
are dominant terms.
Substituting
\gamma_{0}=M^{-e}
and balancing them, we get
N=M^{(\frac{4\alpha}{2\alpha+2\beta-1})(e-1/2)}
.
As
\mathfrak{f}=MN
holds, it leads to

M^{\star}\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{1/((\frac{4\alpha}{2\alpha+2\beta-1})(e-1/2)+1)},\quad R\!\left(M^{\star},\tfrac{\mathfrak{f}}{M^{\star}},\gamma_{0}\right)\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{-\tfrac{(2e-1)(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{2\alpha(2e-1)+(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}}.

For the case
e\geq(\alpha+\beta)
,
\bigl(M^{1/2}N\gamma_{0}\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}}
and
M^{-(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}
are dominant terms.
Substituting
\gamma_{0}=M^{-e}
and balancing them, we get
N=M^{\alpha-\beta+e}
.
As
\mathfrak{f}=MN
holds, it leads to

M^{\star}\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{1/(\alpha-\beta+e+1)},\quad R\!\left(M^{\star},\tfrac{\mathfrak{f}}{M^{\star}},\gamma_{0}\right)\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{\alpha-\beta+e+1}}.

In Figure
21
, we provide a graph of the compute-optimal exponent with respect to
e
of
\gamma_{0}=M^{-e}
for
(\alpha,\beta)=(0.6,0.4)
.
As the graph is continuous, the absolute value of the compute-optimal exponent gradually decreases as we move away from the optimal choice.
Also, we can observe that the degradation is smaller for the learning rates with larger
e
in
\gamma_{0}=M^{-e}
than that of the optimal learning rate.
So in terms of tuning the learning rate, we may aggressively set a high
e
in
\gamma_{0}=M^{-e}
for the initial attempt, and gradually decrease the
e
for later attempts.

Figure 21:
Compute-optimal exponent with respect to
e
of
\gamma_{0}=M^{-e}
for
(\alpha,\beta)=(0.6,0.4)
.

Colored line shows the compute-optimal exponent
x
in the formula
R\!\left(M^{\star},\tfrac{\mathfrak{f}}{M^{\star}},\gamma_{0}\right)\;\eqsim\;\mathfrak{f}^{-x}
.

Appendix G
Derivation for the Stable-decay and Warmup-stable-decay Scheduling

We first derive a scaling-law bound for the stable-decay schedule, a simplified variant of the warmup–stable-decay (WSD) schedule, and then extend it to the full warmup–stable-decay schedule in Section
G.4
.

We set the learning rate as
\gamma_{k}=\gamma_{0}\,f(k)
.
Previously, we considered the constant–learning–rate case (
f\equiv 1
).
In this section, we start with a general decaying learning rate by taking
f
to be a decreasing function, and then substitute the stable-decay scheduling.
Throughout, for simplicity, we assume
\alpha>0.5
and
\beta<0.5
(Phase Aa).

Recall the implicit integral equation (
26
):

\displaystyle L(N)=\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{M}r_{i}(0)\,\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{N}\frac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du\Bigr)

(93)

\displaystyle+\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{z}^{N}\frac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du\Bigr)f(z)^{2}\,dz.

(94)

Also recall Equations
27
and
28
.

L^{\text{drift}}(N)=\sum_{i=1}^{M}r_{i}(0)\,e^{-\tfrac{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{N}\tfrac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du},\quad L^{\text{noise}}(N)=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\int_{0}^{N}e^{-\tfrac{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{z}^{N}\tfrac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du}\,f(z)^{2}\,dz.

(95)

L(N)=\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}+L^{\text{drift}}(N)+L^{\text{noise}}(N).

(96)

Recall also the drift/approximation transformation (
35
):

\displaystyle L^{\text{drift}}(N)+\lVert{\bm{H}}^{1/2}{\bm{w}}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}\;\eqsim\;M^{-(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}\;+\;\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{L}(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}},

\displaystyle Q_{L}(z):=\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{z}\frac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du.

Hence,

\displaystyle L(N)\;\eqsim\;

\displaystyle M^{-(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}\;+\;\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{L}(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}\;

(97)

\displaystyle+\;\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\int_{0}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{z}^{N}\frac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du\Bigr)f(z)^{2}\,dz.

(98)

Remark 2

(Early-iteration proxy)
.

In early iterations the drift term
\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{L}(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}
dominates. Solving

L(N)\eqsim\bigl(M^{0.5}Q_{L}(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2\alpha+2\beta-1}{2\alpha}}

yields

L(N)\;\eqsim\;\bigl(M^{0.5}\gamma_{0}F(N)\bigr)^{-\tfrac{2(2\alpha+2\beta-1)}{\,2\alpha+1-2\beta\,}},\qquad F(N):=\int_{0}^{N}f(u)\,du.

Now we move on to stable-decay scheduling.

Stable-decay schedule.

Figure 22:

Visualization of Stable-decay Scheduling.

For the stable-decay schedule, we set the learning rate to
\gamma_{k}=\gamma_{0}f(k)
with

f(k)=\begin{cases}1,&k\leq pN,\\
\bigl(1+\tau(k-pN)\bigr)^{-c},&k>pN,\end{cases}

(99)

where
p,c\in(0,1)
and
\tau>0
.
In other words, the learning rate remains constant for the first
pN
steps, and then decays polynomially with exponent
c
for the remaining
(1-p)N
steps.

Remark 3
.

Note that
f
depends on the total training steps
N
.
To be precise, we have to represent it as
f_{N}
, but for simplicity, we write it as
f
throughout the analysis.

First, we will make an upper bound on the noise term under stable-decay scheduling.

G.1
Upper Bound of the Noise Term

Fix
p<q<1
close to
1
and split
L^{\text{noise}}(N)
as

\displaystyle L^{\text{noise}}(N)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\int_{0}^{qN}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{z}^{N}\frac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du\Bigr)f(z)^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\int_{qN}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{z}^{N}\frac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du\Bigr)f(z)^{2}\,dz=:T_{\leq qN}+T_{>qN}.

Bounding
T_{>qN}
.

Note that
f(N)\eqsim f(z)
holds for
qN<z<N
. So

\int_{qN}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{z}^{N}\frac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du\Bigr)f(z)^{2}\,dz\;\eqsim\;f(N)^{2}\int_{qN}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{z}^{N}\frac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du\Bigr)dz.

For
q
sufficiently close to
1
, there exist constants
c_{0},c_{1}>0
such that for
qN<z<N

c_{0}\,\frac{(N-z)f(N)}{\sqrt{L(N)}}\;\leq\;\int_{z}^{N}\frac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du\;\leq\;c_{1}\,\frac{(N-z)f(N)}{\sqrt{L(N)}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle T_{>qN}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\,f(N)^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\int_{qN}^{N}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\,c_{0}\,\frac{(N-z)f(N)}{\sqrt{L(N)}}\Bigr)dz

\displaystyle\eqsim\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\,f(N)^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{M}V_{i}\,\frac{\pi\,\sqrt{L(N)}}{4\lambda_{i}\gamma_{0}\,c_{0}\,f(N)}\;\eqsim\;\gamma_{0}\,f(N)\,\sqrt{L(N)}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\frac{V_{i}}{\lambda_{i}}

\displaystyle\eqsim\gamma_{0}\,f(N)\,\sqrt{L(N)}\,\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(\operatorname{diag}(K)^{1/2}\bigr)\;\eqsim\;\gamma_{0}\,f(N)\,\sqrt{L(N)}\,M^{0.5}.

To summarize, we have

T_{>qN}\ \lesssim\ \gamma_{0}\,f(N)\,\sqrt{L(N)}\,M^{0.5}\eqsim\ \gamma_{0}\,M^{1/2}N^{-c}\sqrt{L(N)}.

Bounding
T_{\leq qN}
.

Let
Q(z,N)=\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\int_{z}^{N}\frac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du
.
Then

\displaystyle T_{\leq qN}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}(w_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{K}}_{\sigma}Ku_{i})\int_{0}^{qN}e^{-\frac{4\gamma_{0}}{\pi}\lambda_{i}(\overline{{\bm{K}}})\int_{z}^{N}\frac{f(u)}{\sqrt{L(u)}}\,du}\,f(z)^{2}dz

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\operatorname{Tr}({\bm{K}}_{\sigma}Ku_{i}w_{i}^{\mathsf{T}})\int_{0}^{qN}e^{-\lambda_{i}(\overline{{\bm{K}}})Q(z,N)}\,f(z)^{2}dz

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{qN}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\operatorname{Tr}({\bm{K}}_{\sigma}Ku_{i}w_{i}^{\mathsf{T}})e^{-\lambda_{i}(\overline{{\bm{K}}})Q(z,N)}\,f(z)^{2}dz

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{qN}\operatorname{Tr}({\bm{K}}_{\sigma}K\sum_{i=1}^{M}e^{-\lambda_{i}(\overline{{\bm{K}}})Q(z,N)}u_{i}w_{i}^{\mathsf{T}})\,f(z)^{2}dz

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{qN}\operatorname{Tr}({\bm{K}}_{\sigma}Ke^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}Q(z,N)})\,f(z)^{2}dz.

Using
\arcsin x\approx x
approximation on
{\bm{K}}_{\sigma}=\arcsin(\operatorname{diag}(K)^{-1/2}\cdot K\cdot\operatorname{diag}(K)^{-1/2})
, we get

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}({\bm{K}}_{\sigma}Ke^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}Q(z,N)})=\operatorname{Tr}({\bm{K}}_{\sigma}SH^{1/2}e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(z,N)}H^{1/2}S^{\mathsf{T}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}(H^{1/2}S^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{K}}_{\sigma}SH^{1/2}e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(z,N)})\approx\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}^{2}e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(z,N)}).

Using same contour representation method and deterministic approximation with Section
E.2.2
we get

\displaystyle T_{\leq qN}

\displaystyle\eqsim\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{qN}\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}^{2}e^{-\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}Q(z,N)})\,f(z)^{2}dz

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{qN}\operatorname{Tr}\left(\frac{-1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{2}}z_{1}^{2}e^{-Q(z,N)z_{1}}(\overline{{\bm{K}}}_{1}-z_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-1}\,dz_{1}\right)\,f(z)^{2}dz

\displaystyle\approx\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{qN}\operatorname{Tr}\left(\frac{-1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma}p_{d}^{2}z_{1}^{2}e^{-p_{d}Q(z,N)z_{1}}\mathcal{R}(z_{1})\,dz_{1}\right)\,f(z)^{2}dz

\displaystyle\eqsim\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}M\int_{0}^{qN}\operatorname{Tr}\left(\frac{-1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma}z_{1}^{2}e^{-p_{d}Q(z,N)z_{1}}\mathcal{R}(z_{1})\,dz_{1}\right)\,f(z)^{2}dz

Adopting the method in
32
same as Section
K.1
, we get

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\left(\frac{-1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma}z_{1}^{2}e^{-p_{d}Q(z,N)z_{1}}\mathcal{R}(z_{1})\,dz_{1}\right)\eqsim(p_{d}Q(z,N))^{-2+1/(2\alpha)}\eqsim(M^{1/2}Q(z,N))^{-2+1/(2\alpha)}.

It leads to

\displaystyle T_{\leq qN}

\displaystyle\eqsim\frac{2\gamma_{0}^{2}}{\pi}M\int_{0}^{qN}(M^{1/2}Q(z,N))^{-2+1/(2\alpha)}\,f(z)^{2}dz

\displaystyle\eqsim\gamma_{0}^{2}M^{1/(4\alpha)}\int_{0}^{qN}(Q(z,N))^{-2+1/(2\alpha)}f(z)^{2}\,dz

Finally,

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{2}M^{1/(4\alpha)}\int_{0}^{qN}(Q(z,N))^{-2+1/(2\alpha)}f(z)^{2}\,dz

(100)
